I do maintenance work, and if a breaker snaps, or a tool drops on the floor, or even if somebody just comes up behind me... I jump. And it's not just a quick scare. My heart just keeps hammering in my chest long after everyone else has moved on and gone back to work. It's just getting really hard to deal with.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had an episode where you felt an intense fear or discomfort that reached a peak within minutes?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I get scared and my heart races like I said, but it's always because something happened, like a loud noise or something. I don't just get hit with some random wave of fear out of nowhere, if that's what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or uneasy around other people, even when there aren't any loud noises or sudden events?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, being around people is fine. I work with a crew every day and I don't get nervous just being near them. It's only a problem if... well, like I said, if someone sneaks up behind me or drops something. But just regular talking or, you know, working in the same room as other guys? No, I don't feel uneasy about that at all.\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about things in your daily life besides the fear of being startled?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean... I don't sit around worrying about everyday stuff, if that's what you mean. Just the normal things, I guess, like paying the bills. But I don't stress over it. It's really just the... the being so on edge and jumping at every little thing. That's the only thing that's really getting to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel on edge or anxious even when there are no loud noises or potential startles around you?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, I guess I do. It's like... I'm always waiting for the next loud noise or the next time someone comes up behind me. So even if it's quiet, I'm just... I'm tense. My shoulders are tight, and I'm just waiting for it to happen. I can't really relax, even when nothing is going on.\n\nDoctor: Is this feeling of being on edge something that you experience all the time, even when you're not at work or in a situation where you might be startled?\n\nPatient: Yeah, pretty much. It doesn't matter if I'm at the job site or just sitting in my living room. I'm always kind of... geared up, you know? Just waiting for something to drop or bang or someone to walk in too fast. It's just there all the time.\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about a wide range of everyday things, or is your worry mainly focused on being startled or surprised?\n\nPatient: No, it's really just the... the being startled. Like I said, I don't really stress over normal everyday things. It's just that my mind is always stuck on waiting for a loud noise or for something to jump out at me. That's pretty much the only thing I'm focused on.\n\nDoctor: Is there anything else that you worry about excessively besides being startled?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I don't sit around stressing over normal stuff like bills or work or whatever. I handle all that fine. It's really just... exactly what I was saying. Just my body waiting for a loud bang or someone sneaking up on me. That's the only thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your anxiety is mostly about being startled, or is it more of a general feeling that something bad might happen?\n\nPatient: I mean, like I was just saying, it's really just about being startled. I don't walk around feeling like... like some random bad thing is gonna happen to me or the world is ending. It's literally just my body bracing for a loud noise or a sudden movement. That's all it is.\n\nDoctor: Is your anxiety about being startled something that you think about even when you're not in a situation where you might be startled?\n\nPatient: I mean... I'm not really sure. I haven't really noticed if I sit around thinking about it when I'm just in a normal, quiet spot. I guess I just don't really know if I do or not.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I'm not really sure. I haven't really noticed if I sit around thinking about it when I'm just in a normal, quiet spot. My health, my money, my relationships... that stuff is fine. I don't sit around stressing over everyday things. It's really just the being on guard that I mentioned before.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're able to fully turn off this sense of being on guard when you're in a completely safe environment?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure. That's the only thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your anxiety is mostly about being startled, or is it more of a general feeling that something bad might happen?\n\nPatient: I mean, like I was just saying, it's really just about being startled. I don't walk around feeling like... like some random bad thing is gonna happen to me or the world is ending. It's literally just my body bracing for a loud noise or a sudden movement. That's all it is.\n\nDoctor: Is your anxiety about being startled something that you think about even when you're not in a situation where you might be startled?\n\nPatient: I mean... I'm not really sure. I haven't really noticed if I sit around thinking about it when I'm just in a normal, quiet spot. I guess I just don't really know if I do or not.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you could ever feel completely at ease and relaxed, or is there always some level of tension or anxiety present for you?\n\nPatient: I don't know, honestly. I haven't really thought about it like that. I guess I'm just always a little on edge, you know? Like I said, just waiting for a noise or something. I'm not sure if I ever feel completely relaxed anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself worrying about things even when there's no obvious reason to worry?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Like I said before, it's not a general worry about random things. I don't just sit there worrying about my bills or my health or whatever if there's no reason to. It's really just the physical thing... being on guard for that next loud bang or someone sneaking up on me. That's the only thing that really gets to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious when you're alone in a quiet, safe space?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. If I'm by myself and it's quiet, and I know for sure nobody is around to sneak up on me... I don't feel anxious just from being there. It's not the quiet that bothers me. Like I was saying, it's just the feeling that a loud noise might happen out of nowhere. But if I know it's a safe spot, I don't just sit there feeling nervous for no reason.\n\nDoctor: Do you often worry about everyday things like your health, finances, or relationships?\n\nPatient: No, like I was saying just a minute ago, I don't really have that kind of worry. My health, my money, my relationships... that stuff is fine. I don't sit around stressing over everyday things. It's really just the being on guard that I mentioned before.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're able to fully turn off this sense of being on guard when you're in a completely safe environment?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure. I haven't really thought about if it's, you know, fully turned off. Like I was saying, if I'm somewhere I know is safe and I'm by myself, I don't feel nervous just sitting there. But... I don't know if that means the guard is completely down. I guess I'm not really sure how to answer that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're able to fully relax and not be on guard at any point during the day?\n\nPatient: I honestly couldn't tell you. Like I was just saying, I don't really know if it ever fully turns off. I guess I just don't pay that close attention to whether I'm... you know, completely relaxed during the day. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess what brought me in is... I just can't stop jumping out of my skin. I do maintenance work, and if a breaker snaps, or a tool drops on the floor, or even if somebody just comes up behind me... I jump. And it's not just a quick scare. My heart just keeps hammering in my chest long after everyone else has moved on and gone back to work. It's just getting really hard to deal with.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had an episode where you felt an intense fear or discomfort that reached a peak within minutes?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I get scared and my heart races like I said, but it's always because something happened, like a loud noise or something. I don't just get hit with some random wave of fear out of nowhere, if that's what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or uneasy around other people, even when there aren't any loud noises or sudden events?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, being around people is fine. I work with a crew every day and I don't get nervous just being near them. It's only a problem if... well, like I said, if someone sneaks up behind me or drops something. But just regular talking or, you know, working in the same room as other guys? No, I don't feel uneasy about that at all.\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about things in your daily life besides the fear of being startled?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean... I don't sit around worrying about everyday stuff, if that's what you mean. Just the normal things, I guess, like paying the bills. But I don't stress over it. It's really just the... the being so on edge and jumping at every little thing. That's the only thing that's really getting to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel on edge or anxious even when there are no loud noises or potential startles around you?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, I guess I do. It's like... I'm always waiting for the next loud noise or the next time someone comes up behind me. So even if it's quiet, I'm just... I'm tense. My shoulders are tight, and I'm just waiting for it to happen. I can't really relax, even when nothing is going on.\n\nDoctor: Is this feeling of being on edge something that you experience all the time, even when you're not at work or in a situation where you might be startled?\n\nPatient: Yeah, pretty much. That's all it is.\n\nDoctor: Is your anxiety about being startled something that you think about even when you're not in a situation where you might be startled?\n\nPatient: I mean... From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n18\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF41.1, F41.3\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess what brought me in is... I just can't stop jumping out of my skin. I do maintenance work, and if a breaker snaps, or a tool drops on the floor, or even if somebody just comes up behind me... I jump. And it's not just a quick scare. My heart just keeps hammering in my chest long after everyone else has moved on and gone back to work. It's just getting really hard to deal with.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had an episode where you felt an intense fear or discomfort that reached a peak within minutes?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I get scared and my heart races like I said, but it's always because something happened, like a loud noise or something. I don't just get hit with some random wave of fear out of nowhere, if that's what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or uneasy around other people, even when there aren't any loud noises or sudden events?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, being around people is fine. I work with a crew every day and I don't get nervous just being near them. It's only a problem if... well, like I said, if someone sneaks up behind me or drops something. But just regular talking or, you know, working in the same room as other guys? No, I don't feel uneasy about that at all.\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about things in your daily life besides the fear of being startled?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean... I don't sit around worrying about everyday stuff, if that's what you mean. Just the normal things, I guess, like paying the bills. But I don't stress over it. It's really just the... the being so on edge and jumping at every little thing. That's the only thing that's really getting to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel on edge or anxious even when there are no loud noises or potential startles around you?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, I guess I do. It's like... I'm always waiting for the next loud noise or the next time someone comes up behind me. So even if it's quiet, I'm just... I'm tense. My shoulders are tight, and I'm just waiting for it to happen. I can't really relax, even when nothing is going on.\n\nDoctor: Is this feeling of being on edge something that you experience all the time, even when you're not at work or in a situation where you might be startled?\n\nPatient: Yeah, pretty much. It doesn't matter if I'm at the job site or just sitting in my living room. I'm always kind of... geared up, you know? Just waiting for something to drop or bang or someone to walk in too fast. It's just there all the time.\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about a wide range of everyday things, or is your worry mainly focused on being startled or surprised?\n\nPatient: No, it's really just the... the being startled. Like I said, I don't really stress over normal everyday things. It's just that my mind is always stuck on waiting for a loud noise or for something to jump out at me. That's pretty much the only thing I'm focused on.\n\nDoctor: Is there anything else that you worry about excessively besides being startled?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I don't sit around stressing over normal stuff like bills or work or whatever. I handle all that fine. It's really just... exactly what I was saying. Just my body waiting for a loud bang or someone sneaking up on me. I don't walk around feeling like... like some random bad thing is gonna happen to me or the world is ending. It's literally just my body bracing for a loud noise or a sudden movement. That's all it is.\n\nDoctor: Is your anxiety about being startled something that you think about even when you're not in a situation where you might be startled?\n\nPatient: I mean... I'm not really sure. I haven't really noticed if I sit around thinking about it when I'm just in a normal, quiet spot. I haven't really thought about it like that. I guess I'm just always a little on edge, you know? Like I said, just waiting for a noise or something.     "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n19\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF41.1, F41.3\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess what brought me in is... I just can't stop jumping out of my skin. I do maintenance work, and if a breaker snaps, or a tool drops on the floor, or even if somebody just comes up behind me... I jump. And it's not just a quick scare. My heart just keeps hammering in my chest long after everyone else has moved on and gone back to work. It's just getting really hard to deal with.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had an episode where you felt an intense fear or discomfort that reached a peak within minutes?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I get scared and my heart races like I said, but it's always because something happened, like a loud noise or something. I don't just get hit with some random wave of fear out of nowhere, if that's what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or uneasy around other people, even when there aren't any loud noises or sudden events?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, being around people is fine. I work with a crew every day and I don't get nervous just being near them. It's only a problem if... well, like I said, if someone sneaks up behind me or drops something. But just regular talking or, you know, working in the same room as other guys? No, I don't feel uneasy about that at all.\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about things in your daily life besides the fear of being startled?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean... I don't sit around worrying about everyday stuff, if that's what you mean. Just the normal things, I guess, like paying the bills. But I don't stress over it. It's really just the... the being so on edge and jumping at every little thing. That's the only thing that's really getting to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel on edge or anxious even when there are no loud noises or potential startles around you?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, I guess I do. It's like... I'm always waiting for the next loud noise or the next time someone comes up behind me. So even if it's quiet, I'm just... I'm tense. My shoulders are tight, and I'm just waiting for it to happen. I can't really relax, even when nothing is going on.\n\nDoctor: Is this feeling of being on edge something that you experience all the time, even when you're not at work or in a situation where you might be startled?\n\nPatient: Yeah, pretty much. It doesn't matter if I'm at the job site or just sitting in my living room. I'm always kind of... geared up, you know? Just waiting for something to drop or bang or someone to walk in too fast. It's just there all the time.\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about a wide range of everyday things, or is your worry mainly focused on being startled or surprised?\n\nPatient: No, it's really just the... the being startled. Like I said, I don't really stress over normal everyday things. It's just that my mind is always stuck on waiting for a loud noise or for something to jump out at me. That's pretty much the only thing I'm focused on.\n\nDoctor: Is there anything else that you worry about excessively besides being startled?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I don't sit around stressing over normal stuff like bills or work or whatever. I handle all that fine. It's really just... exactly what I was saying. Just my body waiting for a loud bang or someone sneaking up on me. That's the only thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your anxiety is mostly about being startled, or is it more of a general feeling that something bad might happen?\n\nPatient: I mean, like I was just saying, it's really just about being startled. I don't walk around feeling like... like some random bad thing is gonna happen to me or the world is ending. It's literally just my body bracing for a loud noise or a sudden movement. That's all it is.\n\nDoctor: Is your anxiety about being startled something that you think about even when you're not in a situation where you might be startled?\n\nPatient: I mean... I'm not really sure. I haven't really noticed if I sit around thinking about it when I'm just in a normal, quiet spot. I guess I just don't really know if I do or not.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you could ever feel completely at ease and relaxed, or is there always some level of tension or anxiety present for you?\n\nPatient: I don't know, honestly. I haven't really thought about it like that. I guess I'm just always a little on edge, you know? Like I said, just waiting for a noise or something. I'm not sure if I ever feel completely relaxed anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself worrying about things even when there's no obvious reason to worry?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Like I said before, it's not a general worry about random things. I don't just sit there worrying about my bills or my health or whatever if there's no reason to. It's really just the physical thing... being on guard for that next loud bang or someone sneaking up on me. That's the only thing that really gets to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious when you're alone in a quiet, safe space?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. If I'm by myself and it's quiet, and I know for sure nobody is around to sneak up on me... I don't feel anxious just from being there. It's not the quiet that bothers me. Like I was saying, it's just the feeling that a loud noise might happen out of nowhere. But if I know it's a safe spot, I don't just sit there feeling nervous for no reason.\n\nDoctor: Do you often worry about everyday things like your health, finances, or relationships?\n\nPatient: No, like I was saying just a minute ago, I don't really have that kind of worry. My health, my money, my relationships... that stuff is fine. I don't sit around stressing over everyday things. It's really just the being on guard that I mentioned before.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're able to fully turn off this sense of being on guard when you're in a completely safe environment?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure. I haven't really thought about if it's, you know, fully turned off. Like I was saying, if I'm somewhere I know is safe and I'm by myself, I don't feel nervous just sitting there. But... I don't know if that means the guard is completely down. I guess I'm not really sure how to answer that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're able to fully relax and not be on guard at any point during the day?\n\nPatient: I honestly couldn't tell you. Like I was just saying, I don't really know if it ever fully turns off. I guess I just don't pay that close attention to whether I'm... you know, completely relaxed during the day. I'm usually just working on a job or doing whatever needs to get done. So I'm really not sure.\n\nDoctor: Does being on guard for loud noises or surprises interfere with your daily activities or routines?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it does. None of it really feels worth starting anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're always anxious, even on your best days?\n\nPatient: I don't know if anxious is exactly the right word for it. I don't know, it feels like everybody can just see it on me when they look at me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I don't think it's just about being startled. It's... it's more about avoiding anything that brings it all back. I make excuses at work not to go near certain electrical panels or breakers. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess what brought me in is... I just can't stop jumping out of my skin. I do maintenance work, and if a breaker snaps, or a tool drops on the floor, or even if somebody just comes up behind me... I jump. And it's not just a quick scare. My heart just keeps hammering in my chest long after everyone else has moved on and gone back to work. It's just getting really hard to deal with.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had an episode where you felt an intense fear or discomfort that reached a peak within minutes?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I get scared and my heart races like I said, but it's always because something happened, like a loud noise or something. I don't just get hit with some random wave of fear out of nowhere, if that's what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or uneasy around other people, even when there aren't any loud noises or sudden events?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, being around people is fine. I work with a crew every day and I don't get nervous just being near them. It's only a problem if... well, like I said, if someone sneaks up behind me or drops something. But just regular talking or, you know, working in the same room as other guys? No, I don't feel uneasy about that at all.\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about things in your daily life besides the fear of being startled?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean... I don't sit around worrying about everyday stuff, if that's what you mean. Just the normal things, I guess, like paying the bills. But I don't stress over it. It's really just the... the being so on edge and jumping at every little thing. That's the only thing that's really getting to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel on edge or anxious even when there are no loud noises or potential startles around you?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, I guess I do. It's like... I'm always waiting for the next loud noise or the next time someone comes up behind me. So even if it's quiet, I'm just... I'm tense. My shoulders are tight, and I'm just waiting for it to happen. I can't really relax, even when nothing is going on.\n\nDoctor: Is this feeling of being on edge something that you experience all the time, even when you're not at work or in a situation where you might be startled?\n\nPatient: Yeah, pretty much. It doesn't matter if I'm at the job site or just sitting in my living room. I'm always kind of... geared up, you know? Just waiting for something to drop or bang or someone to walk in too fast. It's just there all the time.\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about a wide range of everyday things, or is your worry mainly focused on being startled or surprised?\n\nPatient: No, it's really just the... the being startled. Like I said, I don't really stress over normal everyday things. It's just that my mind is always stuck on waiting for a loud noise or for something to jump out at me. That's pretty much the only thing I'm focused on.\n\nDoctor: Is there anything else that you worry about excessively besides being startled?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I don't sit around stressing over normal stuff like bills or work or whatever. I handle all that fine. It's really just... exactly what I was saying. Just my body waiting for a loud bang or someone sneaking up on me. That's the only thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your anxiety is mostly about being startled, or is it more of a general feeling that something bad might happen?\n\nPatient: I mean, like I was just saying, it's really just about being startled. I don't walk around feeling like... like some random bad thing is gonna happen to me or the world is ending. It's literally just my body bracing for a loud noise or a sudden movement. That's all it is.\n\nDoctor: Is your anxiety about being startled something that you think about even when you're not in a situation where you might be startled?\n\nPatient: I mean... I'm not really sure. I haven't really noticed if I sit around thinking about it when I'm just in a normal, quiet spot. I guess I just don't really know if I do or not.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you could ever feel completely at ease and relaxed, or is there always some level of tension or anxiety present for you?\n\nPatient: I don't know, honestly. I haven't really thought about it like that. I guess I'm just always a little on edge, you know? Like I said, just waiting for a noise or something. I'm not sure if I ever feel completely relaxed anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself worrying about things even when there's no obvious reason to worry?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Like I said before, it's not a general worry about random things. I don't just sit there worrying about my bills or my health or whatever if there's no reason to. It's really just the physical thing... being on guard for that next loud bang or someone sneaking up on me. That's the only thing that really gets to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious when you're alone in a quiet, safe space?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. If I'm by myself and it's quiet, and I know for sure nobody is around to sneak up on me... I don't feel anxious just from being there. It's not the quiet that bothers me. Like I was saying, it's just the feeling that a loud noise might happen out of nowhere. But if I know it's a safe spot, I don't just sit there feeling nervous for no reason.\n\nDoctor: Do you often worry about everyday things like your health, finances, or relationships?\n\nPatient: No, like I was saying just a minute ago, I don't really have that kind of worry. My health, my money, my relationships... that stuff is fine. I don't sit around stressing over everyday things. It's really just the being on guard that I mentioned before.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're able to fully turn off this sense of being on guard when you're in a completely safe environment?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure. I haven't really thought about if it's, you know, fully turned off. Like I was saying, if I'm somewhere I know is safe and I'm by myself, I don't feel nervous just sitting there. But... I don't know if that means the guard is completely down. I guess I'm not really sure how to answer that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're able to fully relax and not be on guard at any point during the day?\n\nPatient: I honestly couldn't tell you. Like I was just saying, I don't really know if it ever fully turns off. I guess I just don't pay that close attention to whether I'm... you know, completely relaxed during the day. I'm usually just working on a job or doing whatever needs to get done. So I'm really not sure.\n\nDoctor: Does being on guard for loud noises or surprises interfere with your daily activities or routines?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it does. It's really just the being on guard that I mentioned before.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're able to fully turn off this sense of being on guard when you're in a completely safe environment?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure. I haven't really thought about if it's, you know, fully turned off. Like I was saying, if I'm somewhere I know is safe and I'm by myself, I don't feel nervous just sitting there. But... I don't know if that means the guard is completely down. I guess I'm not really sure how to answer that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're able to fully relax and not be on guard at any point during the day?\n\nPatient: I honestly couldn't tell you. Like I was just saying, I don't really know if it ever fully turns off. I guess I just don't pay that close attention to whether I'm... you know, completely relaxed during the day. I'm usually just working on a job or doing whatever needs to get done. So I'm really not sure.\n\nDoctor: Does being on guard for loud noises or surprises interfere with your daily activities or routines?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it does. It's... it's really getting in the way. I've actually been written up at work because of it, and they pulled me out of the running for a supervisor spot. And at home... my wife says I'm barely even present anymore. It's just that everything in my life has started revolving around avoiding another blowup like that day, you know? It just takes over everything.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're on guard and waiting for something to happen even when you're doing things you normally enjoy?\n\nPatient: To be honest, I don't really do the things I used to enjoy anymore. I mean... I used to be the guy staying late to fix up cars, or going out to play ball, or just sticking around to help the young techs learn the ropes. But now... I don't know, I just sit at home. People call, and I just let my phone ring. None of it really feels worth starting anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're always anxious, even on your best days?\n\nPatient: I don't know if anxious is exactly the right word for it. It's more like... I just walk around mad and guilty all the time. Even on a good day, it doesn't go away. I just constantly feel like I failed that kid, and... I don't know, it feels like everybody can just see it on me when they look at me.\n\nDoctor: Is your anxiety something that you think would improve if you could just avoid all situations where you might be startled?\n\nPatient: I don't think it's just about being startled. It's... it's more about avoiding anything that brings it all back. I make excuses at work not to go near certain electrical panels or breakers. I'll avoid specific job sites completely. I don't even want to talk about that day. Because if I get near any of that, or if I start thinking about it... once it starts playing in my head, I just can't shut it off. So I just stay away from all of it."
